# sound glossary: short name, and with the help key, where it fires
cat_sounds=Sound glossary
cat_sounds_help=Every sound of the mod: Confirm plays each one and the info key says what it marks.
snd_npc=Person
snd_npc_help=Repeats while a person is near, coming from their direction: if you hear it on your left, the person is on your left.
snd_object=Object
snd_object_help=An object you can examine nearby: signs, computers, shelves or things on the ground. It plays from its position.
snd_mark=Marker
snd_mark_help=A marker you set yourself with %{key_ctrl} %{key_field}. It plays from its tile, so you can follow it like a radar blip.
snd_scene=Scene trigger
snd_scene_help=Plays from an invisible tile that starts a scene when you step on or touch it, if you turn on scene triggers in the locator.
snd_door=Door or exit
snd_door_help=A door or a map exit near you, from its position.
snd_teleporter=Teleporter
snd_teleporter_help=A teleporter, portal or elevator near you, from its position.
snd_control=Lever or switch
snd_control_help=A puzzle control nearby: levers, buttons and switches that change something on the map.
snd_push=Pushable block
snd_push_help=A block or boulder you can push, from its position.
snd_trap=Moving obstacle
snd_trap_help=A puzzle obstacle that moves: moving platforms or things that patrol and can reach you.
snd_hazard=Hazard
snd_hazard_help=A puzzle hazard, such as a damaging wall, a beam or spikes, from its position.
snd_belt=Conveyor
snd_belt_help=A conveyor belt or tile that carries you when you step on it, from its position.
snd_exit=Edge exit
snd_exit_help=Where the map's edge crosses into another map: it sounds from the nearest crossing tile of each destination.
snd_jump=Jump
snd_jump_help=With the guide on, the route jumps a ledge from here: it sounds toward the jump.
snd_ledge=Ledge
snd_ledge_help=There is a ledge you can jump right in front of you.
snd_grid=Grid cell
snd_grid_help=On a puzzle grid, the tone of the cell you are on: the column sounds left, centre or right, and the row high at the top and low at the bottom.
snd_water=Water nearby
snd_water_help=Continuous loop of the water near you: rivers, sea or ponds. On land it tells you where the water is; while you surf it plays around you, and the shore has its own cue.
snd_shore=Shore
snd_shore_help=A soft, slow footstep in grass while surfing: it plays from the nearest land you can get off onto. With none within the sonar's range, it is silent.
snd_wind_n=Wall wind to the north
snd_wind_e=Wall wind to the east
snd_wind_s=Wall wind to the south
snd_wind_w=Wall wind to the west
snd_wind_help=Wind loop marking a long wall on that side; the louder it is, the closer the wall. Each side has its own wind so two walls at once do not blur into one mass of noise.
snd_wall=Wall bump
snd_wall_help=Plays when you walk into a wall. You hear it on the side you bumped into: the same sound shifted left, center or right.
snd_interact=Bump into something usable
snd_interact_help=Plays when you bump into something you can actually interact with: a sign, a person or an object. Different from the wall bump so you know something is there.
snd_bump=Plain bump
snd_bump_help=The simple bump sound, used when positional audio is not available.
snd_step=Footstep
snd_step_help=Your own footstep on normal ground.
snd_grass=Footstep in grass
snd_grass_help=Your footstep in tall grass, where wild Pokémon can appear.
snd_swim=Footstep in water
snd_swim_help=Your footstep while surfing or moving through water.
snd_guide=Guide
snd_guide_help=The guide: it plays while you follow a target, shifted toward the side of the route's next step. Here you hear it centered.
snd_guide_hold=Guide, keep the key held down
snd_guide_hold_help=The same guide sound, but continuous: it plays toward the next step while that step is on ground where you have to keep the key held down, and stops once you leave that ground behind. Here you hear it centered.
snd_radar=Proximity radar
snd_radar_help=A short beep when something you can interact with is right in front of you.
snd_minigame=Minigame tick
snd_minigame_help=Marks the rhythm or the closeness inside a minigame: the higher the pitch, the closer to the sweet spot.

# setting help
help_language=The mod voice language. Automatic takes the system's; if the mod lacks that one, the language the game declares; and failing that, English.
help_auto_guide=Starts the audio guide to a target as soon as you select it.
help_auto_steps=Starts the spoken step-by-step guide to a target as soon as you select it.
help_hide_unreachable=Which targets the list leaves out. With no route: those you cannot walk to. With no route or in other rooms: also, on a map that packs several rooms, those outside yours, as long as yours has any. In other rooms: only the latter; the ones in your room stay even with no route. In all three, except in the fixed order, the ones in your room come first and the rest follow by how many rooms away they are.
hide_off=none
hide_unreachable=with no route
hide_room=with no route or in other rooms
hide_other_rooms=in other rooms
help_hide_noninter=Leaves out decorative events with no interaction (background lights and animations, sprites with no dialogue). Handy if they clutter your search; keep it off if a game needs you to locate them.
help_scene_triggers=Locates the invisible tiles that start a scene when you step on or touch them, in a category and with a sound of their own; a row of them counts as one. Off by default: knowing where a scene waits gives part of it away.
help_everything_extras=Adds the targets of puzzles, surfaces and your markers to the Everything category, each once. Off by default: Everything gets longer.
help_name_items=Says the name of the item inside a Poké Ball on the ground, instead of a generic object.
help_name_trainers=Says the class of a trainer with no name of its own, such as Hiker, and whether it has been beaten, instead of a generic trainer.
help_name_species=Says the species of a Pokémon drawn on the map, such as Growlithe, instead of the file name of its picture.
help_name_signs=Says what an invisible event with text is by what that text says, such as a bin, a shelf or a locked door, instead of a generic sign.
help_route_style=Which route is chosen when there are several. The shortest: the fewest steps and, between two equal ones, the fewest turns. Balanced: one extra step is accepted for each turn it saves. The straightest: the fewest turns, even if longer, up to a quarter more plus 4 steps; past that, the balanced one. The fewer the turns, the fewer directions to follow.
help_reach=How far, in tiles counting horizontal plus vertical, targets are found and routed to. The farther, the more targets are reached, though long routes may run into the pathfinder limit.
help_astar=How many tiles the pathfinder may examine in each search. Higher finds longer or more winding routes but takes a little longer.
lbl_path_algorithm=Route algorithm
algo_astar=A-star
algo_weighted=Weighted A-star
algo_greedy=Greedy, beeline
algo_dijkstra=Dijkstra
algo_bfs=Breadth-first, BFS
algo_dfs=Depth-first, DFS
algo_jps=Jump, JPS
algo_hpa=Hierarchical, HPA-star
algo_regions=Regions
help_algo_astar=A-star: the shortest, optimal route, searched afresh every time. Regions uses it until it knows the map.
help_algo_weighted=Weighted A-star: explores fewer tiles, a bit faster, but the route may not be the shortest.
help_algo_greedy=Greedy: heads straight for the target, ignoring distant obstacles. Quick to compute but often detours.
help_algo_dijkstra=Dijkstra: optimal like A-star but with no heuristic, so it explores more tiles. Same route, just slower.
help_algo_bfs=Breadth-first: explores level by level. Optimal in number of steps, like A-star but slower.
help_algo_dfs=Depth-first: dives deep down one path before trying others. Long, winding routes. For experimenting only.
help_algo_jps=Jump (JPS): a faster A-star that leaps along straight corridors, exploring fewer tiles. The route takes as few steps as A-star's, though among routes as short it may pick one with more turns. It only works with the preferred route set to the shortest; with the others, A-star is used.
help_algo_hpa=Hierarchical (HPA-star): splits the map into blocks and routes block by block. It is approximate: the route can be much longer than the shortest, and here it is not faster than A-star. On maps with ledges, ice or events that move the player it uses A-star.
help_algo_regions=Regions: once the pathfinder knows the whole map, it works out the route from what it learned without asking the game again: the same routes as A-star, faster, and never cut short on huge maps. Until it knows the map it uses A-star, and if A-star runs out of budget, it keeps exploring the map and gives the route. The recommended option; works well with "Explore the map in the background".
lbl_edge_relax=Lenient map edges
help_edge_relax=If an exit or floor on the map border reads as inaccessible (detected as a wall), turn this on to reach it. Experimental, off by default.
lbl_regions_background=Explore the map in the background
help_regions_background=After entering a map or when an event ends, the pathfinder explores the map a little on every frame, so routes and hidden targets answer at once on big maps too. While it lasts, games that already run at their limit may lose a little smoothness; on small maps it finishes almost at once. If you notice stutters, turn this option off.
lbl_regions_slice=Time per frame
help_regions_slice=How long each frame may spend exploring the map while the character stands still. With more time it finishes sooner; if you notice stutters, lower it. While walking it never goes over 2 milliseconds.
lbl_neighbour_cache=Keep neighboring maps
help_neighbour_cache=To tell whether the player can leave the map from a border tile, the game loads the whole neighboring map every time it is asked. With this option a copy of each neighboring map is kept, so the target list answers at once on maps connected to others. The answers are the same. It uses a little more memory; turn it off only if something seems wrong when leaving a map by its edge.
lbl_neighbours_background=Load neighboring maps in the background
help_neighbours_background=While the player stands still, the neighboring maps are loaded one by one, so the first press on each map answers at once too. Each load may be felt as a small stutter, which is why this is off by default.
maps_unit.one=map
maps_unit.other=maps
lbl_neighbour_cache_size=Neighboring maps to keep
help_neighbour_cache_size=How many neighboring maps are kept at once. Past this number the one unused the longest is dropped, and loaded again if it is needed. With more, less has to be loaded when returning to an area; it uses a little more memory.
lbl_guide_distance=Guide distance
help_guide_distance=How many tiles to your left or right the guide (and the jump) plays from when the step goes that way; up and down it plays where you stand, higher or lower. Closer feels more immediate; farther stops the sound from sliding to the other ear when you walk fast.
lbl_route_limit=Pathfinder limit
rlim_effort=by effort
rlim_time=by time
rlim_none=none
help_route_limit=How far the pathfinder keeps trying before it gives up. By effort: until it has examined a set number of tiles. By time: until a few milliseconds run out, those of Maximum search time; any stutter is bounded by that setting, but on big maps a distant target may be left without a route. None: it does not give up until it finds one; on big maps you may feel a stutter.
lbl_route_budget=Maximum search time
help_route_budget=How many milliseconds at most each part of a route search may take: the search itself and, when needed, the regions on foot, assisted and by water, each with this time. Higher finds longer routes but may cause a small stutter.
lbl_astar_weight=Weighted A-star weight
help_astar_weight=How much the distance still to go counts against the way already walked in weighted A-star. A higher weight explores fewer tiles and answers sooner, but the route can stray further from the shortest.
aweight_w125=1.25
aweight_w150=1.5
aweight_w200=2
aweight_w300=3
lbl_hpa_cluster=HPA-star block size
help_hpa_cluster=How many tiles across each block is when HPA-star splits the map. Bigger blocks bring the route closer to the shortest, but each search examines more tiles.
help_footstep_vol=Volume of each footstep while walking.
help_wall_vol=Volume of the cue when you bump or push against a wall.
help_interact_vol=Volume of the cue when you bump into something you can interact with: a sign, a person or an object.
help_game_bump=Also plays the game's own bump sound when you walk into a wall. When off, only the mod's wall cue is heard while that cue is on.
help_guide_vol=Volume of the guide that points to the target.
help_guide_freq=How often the guide chime toward the target plays. Higher is more frequent.
help_guide_rhythm=How the rhythm of the guide's chimes changes. By distance: further apart the further away the target is, up to twice what the frequency sets. Constant: always at the guide's frequency. Either way, it chimes at once when the next step's direction changes.
help_sonar_locatable=When on, the sonar only plays for what the locator keys can reach. When off (the default), it plays for everything around you, including events that trigger when you step on them, which the locator keys do not list.
help_surface_cues=Announces the terrain under you when it changes: grass, water, sand, ice.
help_pos_master=Master volume of positional audio.
help_pos_output=How placed sounds reach your ears or speakers. Binaural: HRTF, made for headphones. Stereo: each sound on its side, higher ahead and lower behind; better on speakers or if HRTF sounds odd. Mono: a single channel, no left or right; the pitch tells ahead from behind. Quadraphonic, 5.1 and 7.1: for those speakers, which must also be set up in Windows; what is behind comes from the rear ones.
help_audio3d_pitch=In stereo and mono, how high sounds play by where they come from, in percent of the original sound: above 100 is higher and below is lower. North is up on the map; a sound on a diagonal mixes both sides. It adds to each sound's own pitch.
help_pos_people=Volume of positional people cues.
help_pos_objects=Volume of positional object cues.
help_pos_marks=Volume of the positional cues of your markers.
help_pos_scenes=Volume of the positional cues of scene triggers.
help_pos_doors=Volume of positional door and exit cues.
help_pos_water=Volume of positional water sound.
help_pos_shore=Volume of the soft cue that, while surfing, plays from the nearest land you can get off onto.
help_pos_wind=Volume of the positional wind marking walls.
help_freq_people=How often positional people cues play.
help_freq_objects=How often positional object cues play.
help_freq_marks=How often the positional cues of your markers play.
help_freq_scenes=How often the positional cues of scene triggers play.
help_freq_doors=How often positional door and exit cues play.
help_tone_people=Pitch of the positional people cues: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_tone_objects=Pitch of the positional object cues: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_tone_marks=Pitch of the positional cues of your markers: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_tone_scenes=Pitch of the positional cues of scene triggers: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_tone_doors=Pitch of the positional door and exit cues: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_tone_teleporter=Pitch of the sound that marks teleporters and elevators: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_on_guide=The sound guide toward the selected target, the same as %{key_shift} %{key_route}: the row says whether it is on and turns it on or off. It is not saved between sessions, and it starts sounding once the menu closes.
help_pos_hazards=Volume of positional hazard cues: damaging walls, beams or spikes in a puzzle.
help_freq_hazards=How often positional hazard cues play.
help_tone_hazards=Pitch of the positional hazard cues: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_pos_traps=Volume of positional trap cues: puzzle obstacles that move and can reach you.
help_freq_traps=How often positional trap cues play.
help_tone_traps=Pitch of the positional trap cues: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_pos_controls=Volume of positional control cues: levers, buttons and switches in a puzzle.
help_freq_controls=How often positional control cues play.
help_tone_controls=Pitch of the positional control cues: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_pos_pushes=Volume of positional cues of blocks and boulders you can push.
help_freq_pushes=How often positional cues of pushable blocks play.
help_tone_pushes=Pitch of the positional cues of pushable blocks: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_pos_belts=Volume of positional conveyor cues: conveyor belts and tiles that carry you when you step on them.
help_freq_belts=How often positional conveyor cues play.
help_tone_belts=Pitch of the positional conveyor cues: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_pos_exits=Volume of positional edge exit cues: where the map's edge crosses into another map.
help_freq_exits=How often positional edge exit cues play.
help_tone_exits=Pitch of the positional edge exit cues: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_freq_teleporter=How often positional teleporter and elevator cues play.
help_jump_vol=Volume of the jump sound, which marks with the guide on the tile where the route jumps a ledge, and of the ledge cue.
help_jump_tone=Pitch of the jump sound and the ledge cue: 50 is the original. It goes down almost half an octave but up only a little, so that ahead (high) and behind (low) stay apart.
help_radar_vol=Volume of the proximity radar's beep, which plays when something you can interact with is right in front of you.
help_radar_tone=Pitch of the proximity radar's beep: 50 is the original, lower than the guide ahead so the two are not confused. Less makes it lower and more, higher.
help_puzzle_vol=Volume of each cell's tone as you move over a puzzle grid.
help_grid_tone=Pitch of a puzzle grid's cells: 50 is the original sound. It goes down almost an octave but barely up, so that the top row (high) and the bottom row (low) stay apart.
help_minigame_vol=Volume of the tick that marks the rhythm or the closeness in minigames, and of the arrows in Realidea's rhythm game; with the minigame sound off or at 0, those arrows are spoken.
help_tone_water=Pitch of the positional water sound: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_tone_wind=Pitch of the positional wind that marks the walls: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_footstep_tone=Pitch of the sound of each step while walking and of the shore cue: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_wall_tone=Pitch of the cue when bumping or pushing against a wall: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_interact_tone=Pitch of the cue when you bump into something you can interact with: 50 is the original sound, lower makes it deeper and higher makes it sharper, up to an octave each way.
help_guide_tone=Pitch of the guide sound that points to the target: 50 is the original. It goes down almost half an octave but up only a little, so that ahead (high) and behind (low) stay apart.
help_wall_range=How many tiles away walls are detected for the wind sound.
help_wall_falloff=How much the wall wind drops with distance. Higher makes only the wall right beside you sound, and makes narrow openings clearer.
help_desk_range=How many tiles away counter NPCs (nurse, mart, PC) stay audible through the wall, so you can find them across the desk. At 0 they are hidden too.
help_sonar_range=How many tiles away people and objects are detected for their positional cues.
help_alt_dist=How close (in tiles) two positional cues must be to play one after another instead of at once, so you can tell them apart. Higher makes more of them take turns.
help_occlusion=What to do with people, objects or doors behind a wall: hear normally, muffle (you hear them dampened, so you know something is back there) or hide (silent). Muffle keeps the direction intact.
help_pos_air=Distant sounds lose treble, as an extra distance cue. Does not affect direction or precision.

# grid-tile puzzles
lbl_puzzle_assist=Puzzle assist
help_puzzle_assist=When on, you get more information and hints to solve puzzles, such as what each control would open or close and how far a piece still is from its goal. Routes can also plan pushing several Strength boulders and take you first to the plate or lever that opens the way, and the puzzles category offers a piece's goal with the route that pushes it there. With it, routes also keep off traps that cannot be seen. When off, you only hear the minimum: your position and each element's state; a route only has you push the boulder that blocks the way, and when the way depends on a control, there is no route.

vbh_party=Brief: name, HP, status condition or fainted, and able or not able. Medium: the level as well. Full: everything, including the species if nicknamed, gender, shiny, Pokérus, held item and markings.
vbh_battle_move=Brief: name and PP. Medium: the type as well. Full: everything, including the category and, depending on the game, power and accuracy.
vbh_battle_marks=The icons a Pokémon's battle box draws beside its name: shiny, caught, Mega Evolved, Primal, and each game's own. Brief does not say them. Medium and Full say them once as each foe comes in and when checking HP. The info key always says them.
vbh_bes_battler=Brief: who it is, its HP and status, the stat changes and the effects on it. Medium: its types, ability, item, last move and the weather as well. Full: the turn as well.

bt_mark_primal=Primal
bt_marks_entry=%{name}: %{marks}
vbh_summary_move=Brief: name and PP. Medium: the type as well. Full: everything, including category, power, accuracy and description.
vbh_learn_move=Brief: the name only. Medium: the type as well and, in games that charge for teaching it, what it costs. Full: everything, including category, power, accuracy, PP and description.
vbh_bag_item=Brief: the item, its quantity and whether it is being moved. Medium and Full: its marks as well, such as registered, and what the screen shows of it, such as its flavor or level, or which team members can use it.
vbh_shop_item=Brief: the item and its price. Medium and Full: how many are in the bag, its marks and what an upgrade gives as well.
vbh_pc_slot=Brief: name, level and whether it has fainted. Medium: where it is and its held item as well. Full: everything, including gender, shiny, types, ability and markings.
vbh_dex_entry=Brief: number and name. Medium: whether it was caught or only seen as well, and what the list adds, such as its rarity or a recipe's stars. Full: everything.
vbh_positions=Where a row is in its list, such as 3 of 10, and which page a screen of several is on. Brief leaves it out; Medium and Full say it.
vbh_hints=The keys a screen points out, such as press Z for its description. Brief leaves them out; Medium and Full say them.
vbh_dex_page=Brief: number, name and whether it was caught. Medium: the category, types and abilities as well. Full: everything, including height, weight, description and base stats.
vbh_ribbon=Brief: the name of the ribbon or memento, or that the slot is empty. Medium: where it is as well. Full: everything, including its description.
vbh_descriptions=What a screen explains about the focused option: a menu's help, a challenge's rule, an achievement's description, the effect of a talisman or a cheer, a difficulty, the detail of a place on the region map. Also a puzzle room's rule on entering it, such as a gym's trap tiles. Brief and Medium leave it out; Full says it. The info key always says it. Menu help is also silenced at every level when Read menu help is off.
vbh_room_note=When a target picked in the locator lies in another room of the same map, says so: "in another room", or "2 rooms away" when more lie between. Brief leaves it out; Medium and Full say it.
vbh_route_transfers=On the route line and in the step guide, says where a warp to another spot of the same map is crossed: "3 up, warp, 2 right". Brief leaves it out; Medium and Full say it.
vbh_teleport=When the game moves you at once to another spot of the same map, as a teleport does. Brief: only that you moved. Medium: also how far and which way, "5 up and 3 to the left". Full: also which part of the map you are in now, "the northeast part of the map".
vbh_map_changes=What a mechanism you used opens or closes, such as a lever or a plate. Brief: not said. Medium: what happened, "A way has opened". Full: also where, "4 right, 2 up".
vbh_quest=Brief: the name of the quest or challenge. Medium: its state (completed or not, reward ready) and its marks (main, new) as well. Full: everything, including a challenge's reward.
vbh_map_square=Brief: the place or tile, whether you can fly or place something there, which decoration is there while removing and whether it is unvisited. Medium: the point of interest, the decoration being placed or the coordinates as well. Full: everything, including the tile's description.
vbh_hall_of_fame=Brief: the Pokémon, its nickname and, when moving to another entry, which one it is. Medium: its level as well. Full: everything the card shows, such as gender, original trainer and moves.
vbh_pokemon_choice=Brief: the name. Medium: its types or category as well, where the screen shows them. Full: everything its card shows, such as gender, ability, moves and held item.
vbh_rem_blessing=Brief: what the card does. Medium: its rarity as well. Full: everything, including its category.
vbh_incubator=Brief and Medium: the slot, whether it holds an egg and the steps it has left. Full: the screen's sentence on how close it is to hatching as well.
